THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JULY 13, 1974/ASADHA 22, 1896 Part II— 


(Department of Expenditure) 
New Delhi, the 1st July, 1974 


S.O. .1728, — In exercise of the powers conferred by the 
proviso to article 309 and clause (5) of article 148 of the 
Constitution, and of all other powers enabling him in this 
behalf, the President, after consultation with the Comptrol- 
ler and Auditor-General of India in respect of persons ser- 
ving in the Indian Audit and Accounts Department, hereby 
makes the following rules further to amend the General 
Provident Fund (Central Services) Rules, 1960, namely: — 
1. (1) These rules may be called the General Provident 
Fund (Central Services) (Fifth Amendment) Rules, 1974. 

(2) They shall come into force on the date of their pub- 
lication in the Offial Gazette. 


2, In the General Provident Fund (Central Services) 
Rules, 1960, for sub-nile (3) of rule 16, the following iub- 
rulc shall be substituted, namely: — 

“(3) (a) A subscriber who has been permitted under 
clause (d) clause (c) or clause (f) of sub-rule (i) of 15 to 
withdraw money from the amount standing to his credit in 
the Fund, shall not part with the possession of the house 
built or acquired or house-site purchased with the money so 
withdrawn, whether by way of sale, mortgage (other than 
mortgage to the President) gift, exchange or otherwise, with- 
out the previous permission of the President: 

Provided that such permission shall not be necessary for: 

(i) the house or house-site being leased for any term 

not exceeding three years, or 

(ii) its being mortgaged in favour of a Housing Road, 
the Life Insurance Corporation or any other cor- 
poration owiied or controlled bv the Central Gov- 
ernment which advances loans for the construction 
of a new house or for making additibns or altera- 
tions to an existing house. 

(b) The subscriber shall submit a declaration not later 
than the 31st day of December of every year as to whether 
the house or the house-site, as the case may be, continues to 
be in his possession or has been mortgaged, otherwise trans- 
ferred or let out as aforesaid and shall, if so required, pro- 
duce before the sanctioning authority on or before the date 
specified by that authority in that behalf, the original sale, 
mortgage or lease deed and also the documents on which 
his title to the property is based. 

(c) If, at any time before his retirement, the subscriber 
parts with the possession of the house or house-site without 
obtaining the previous permission of the President, he shall 
forthwith repay the sum so withdrawn by him in a lump 
sum to the Fund, and, in default of such repayment, the 
sanctioning authority shall, after giving the subscriber a 
reasonable opportunity of making a representation in the 
matter, cause the said sum to be recovered from the emo- 
luments of the subscriber either In a lump sum or in such 
number of monthly instalments, as may be determined by 
it”. 


tNo. 13(l)-EV(B)/73-GPF] 


New Delhi, the 28th June, 1974 


S.O. 1729.— In exercise of the powers conferred by the 
proviso to article 309 and clause (5) of article 148 of the 
Constitution, and of all other powers enabling him in this 
behalf, the President, after consultation with the Comp- 
troller and Auditor-General of India in respect of persons 
serving in the Indian Audit and Accounts Department, here- 
by makes the following rules further to amend the Contri- 
butory Provident Fund Rules (India), 1962, namely: — 

1. (1) These rules may be called the Contributory Provi- 
dent Fund (India) (Second Amendment) Rules, 1974. 

(2) They shall come Into force on the date of their pub- 
lication in the Official Gazette. 

2. In the Contributory Provident Fund Rules (India), 
1962, for sub-rule (3) of rule 17, the following sub-rule 
shall be substituted, namely.-- 

"(3)(a) A subscriber who has been permitted under 
clause (d), clause (e) or clause (f) of sub-rule (1) 
of rule 16 to withdraw money from the amount of 
Subscription together with interest thereon standing 
to his credit in the Fund, shall not part with *hc 
possession of the house built or acquired or house- 
site purchased with the money so withdrawn, whe- 


ther by way of sale, mortgage (other than mortgage 
to the President) gift, exchange or otherwise without 
the previous permission of the President ; 

Provided that such permission shall not be necessary 
for — 

(i) the house or house-site being leased for any term 

not exceeding three years, or 

(ii) its being mortgaged in favour of a Housing 

Board, the Life Insurance Corporation or any 
other corporation owned or controlled by the 
Central Government which advances loans for the 
construction of a new house or for making addi- 
tions or alterations to an existing house. 

(b) The subscriber shall submit a declaration not later 

than the 31st day of December of every year as 
to whether the house or the house-site, as 
the case may be, continues to be In his 
possession or has been mortgaged, otherwise trans- 
ferred or let out as aforesaid and shrill, if so re- 
quired, produce before the sanctioning authority 
on or before the date specified by that authority 
in that behalf, the original sale, mortgage or lease 
deed and also the documents on which his title to 
the property is based. 

(c) If at any time before his retirement, the subscriber 
parts with the possession of the house" or house- 
site without obtaining the previous permission of 
the President, he shall forthwith repay the sum so 
withdrawn by him in a lump sum to the Fund, and, 
in default of such repayment, the sanctioning autho- 
rity shall, after giving the subscriber a reasonable 
opportunity of making a representation in the 
matter, cause the said sum to be recovered from the 
emoluments of the subscriber either in a lump sum 
or in such number of monthly instalments, as may 
be determined by it.” 

[No. 13(l)-EV(B)/73-CPF] 
S. S. L, MALHOTRA, Under Secy.

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM AND CHEMICALS 
(Department of Petroleum) 

New Delhi, the 29th June, 1974 

S. O. 1736. — ’Whereas it appears to the Central Govern- 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of petroleum from Sanand-17 to Sanand-27 in 
Gujarat State Pipelines should be laid by the Oil and Natu- 
tal Gas Commission; 

And whereas it appears that for the- purpose of laying 
such Pipelines, It is necessary to acquire the Right of User 
in the land described In the schedule annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (I) of the section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines 
(Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 1962 (50 of 
1962), the Central Government hereby declares its intention 
to acquiie the right of user therein; 

Provided -that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 



1746 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JULY 

the laying of the pipelines under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Construction 
and Maintenance Division, Makarpum Road, Baroda-9. 

And every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person 
or by a legal practitioner.

S.O- 1739. - Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that coal is likely to be obtained from the lands mentioned 
in the Schedule hereto annexed; 



Sec. 3 fii) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : 


Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (1) of section 4 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition 
and Development) Act, 1957 20 of 1957), the Central Govern- 
ment hereby gives notice of its intention to prospect for 
coal therein. 

The plan of the area covered by this notification may be 
inspected in the office of the National Coal Development Cor- 
poration Limited (Revenue Section). Darbhanga House, Ranchi 
(Bihar), or in the office of the Collector, Burdwan (West Bengal 
or in the office of the Coal Controller, 1-Council House Street, 
Calcutta. 

Any person interested in the lands mentioned in the Scheduled 
to the notification shall deliver all maps, charts and other docu- 
ments referred to in sub-section (7) of section 13 of the said Act 
to the Revenue Officer of the National Coal Development 
Corporation Limited, Darbhanga House, Ranchi (Bihar) within 
ninety days from the date of the publication of this notification 
in the Gazette of India, as required by rule 5 of the Coal Bearing 
Areas (Acquisition and Development) Rules, 1957.

New Delhi, the 25lh .Tunc, 1974 


S.O. 1740 — WHEREAS it appears to the Central Government 
that coal is likely to be obtained from the lands mentioned in the 
Schedule hereto annexed; 


NOW, THERL'FORF, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (1) of section 4 of the Coal Rearing Areas (Acquisi- 
tion and Development) Ac f , 1957 (20 of 1957), the Central Govern- 
ment hereby gives notice of its intention to prospect for coal 
therein, 


The plan of the area covered by his notification can be 
inspected at the office of the National Coal Development Cor- 
poration Limited (Revenue Section), Daibhanga House, Ranchi 
or at the ofTlco of the Dy. Cornmissionei , Hazaribagb (Bihar), 
the Deputy Commissioner, Giridih (Bihar), the Deputy Com- 
missioner, Dlmnbad or at the office of the Coat Conti oiler, !, 
Council House Street, Calcutta. 


All persons interested in the land covered by this notification 
shall deliver alt maps, charts and other documents referred to in 
sub-section (7) of section 13 of the said Act to the Revenue Officer . 
National Coal Development Corporation Limited, Darbhanga 
House, Ranchi within 90 days from the date of publication of 
this notification.

PRESENT: 

Shii K. K. Sarknr, Judge, Presiding Offieci. 

Reference No. 10 of 1972 

In the mattci of an industrial dispute u|s 10(1) (d) of 
the Industrial Disputes \ct, 1947. 

PARTIES: fctnplotcis in lclation to the management of 
Balihan Collieiy of Messrs Bhaiat Coking Coal 
Limited, Post Office kusunda, Distnct Dhanbad 

AND 

Their workmen. 

APPEARANCES: 

On behalf of the employers : Shri S. Mukhcijcc, 
Advocate. 

On behalf of the woikmen: Shri J. D. Lai, Advocate. 

State: Bihar. Industry. Coal. 

Dhanbad, the 22nd June, 1974 
AWARD 

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour and Reha- 
bilitation in the Department of Labour & Employment is of 
opinion that an industnal dispute exists between the em- 
ployets in relation to the management of Balihan Collieiy 
of Messrs Bharat Coking Coal Limited, Post Office Is.ii- 
sunda, Distnct Dhanbad and then woikmen. Accoidingly 
they by older o. L|2012|30|72-LRTI dated 4th September, 
1972 referred the said dispute to this Tnbunul u/s 10(1) 
(d) of the I.D. Act, 1947 for adjudication upon the issue 
as in the schedule below: 


SCHEDULE 

“Whether the action of the management of Bahhari 
Colliery, post office Kusunda, District Dhanbad 
at present under the management of Messts Bharat 
Coking Coal Limited, in stopping Sarvaslm Ram 
Khclawan Bhuiyan, Sudesh Bhuiyan, Kunhaiyu 
Bhuiyan, Triloki Bhuiyan and Tctar Bhuiyan, Sur- 
face Trammers from work with effect fiom the 
18th Novembei, 1971, is justified? ff not, to what 
relief are the workmen entitled?" 


The parties duly enteied their appearance and tiled theii 
vyntten statements in due couise. The paities then wanted 
time for filing documents and the lelerencc diagged on for 
filing such documents. Then at one stage the paities want- 
ed time to file memorandum of settlement. Such time was 
allowed and the reference dragged on for filing settlement, 
ultimately, on 21-6-74 the parties filed a joint petition of 
compromise, incoi pointing therein the teims of settlement. 
1 heard the learned Advocates representing both sides on 
the joint petition of compromise and it is submitted that 
the dispute has been finally resolved between the paities 
and an nward be passed in accordance thereof. 1 have gone 
through the teims of settlement which appeal to have 
been properly signed by Shri R. D. P. Sinlin, Acting Sub 
Area Manager, Bhagaband Sub Area No. XIII and by 
Shri S. S. Mukherjee, Advocate representmg the manage- 
ment on the one hand and by Shri J, D. Lai, Advocate and 
Secretary, Bihar Collieiy Kamgarh Union leprcscnting the 
workmen. The teims of the settlement ate beneficial to 
both sides. There is no i cason why the same should not 
be accepted. Accordingly I accopt the terms of settlement 
as incorporated in the joint petition of compromise. 

In the result I make an award in this case in teims of 
the settlement as incorporated in the joint petition ot com- 
promise which do form pait of the uwaid as Annexing A. 

Sd 


ANNEXURE A 

BEFORE THE PRFSIDING OFFICER,

In the matter of Ref. No. 10 of 1972 
PARTIES: Employeis in relation to Balihan Colliery. 
AND 

Their workmen. 

PETITION OF COMPROMISE 

The humble petition on behalf of the parties to the dis- 
pute most respectfully herewith : — 

(1) That without prejudice to the respective conten- 
tions of the parties, the dispute has been amicably 
settled on the following terms; — 

TERMS Ob SFTTLEMENT 

(a) That the concerned workmen S/Shri Ramkhelwan 
Bhuiyan, Sudesh Bhuiyan, Kanhaiya Bhuiyan, Tri-

AWARD 

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour and Reha- 
bilitation in the Department of Labour & Employment is 
of opinion that an industrial dispute exists between the 
employers in relation to the management of National Coal 
Development Corporation Limited, Ranchi and their work- 
men and accordingly they referred the said industrial dispute 
u/s 10(1) (d) of the T.D. Act, 1947 to this Tribunal by 
their order No. 1 /2012/135/71-LR. II dated 15-12-1971 for 
adjudication upon the issue as mentioned in the schedule 

below ■ 

SCHEDULE 

“Whether the employers in relation to the Kathara 
Washery of Messrs. National Coal Development 
Coiporation Limited, was justified in reverting Shri 
lai Narayan Singh, Work Mistry from the post of 
work supervisor ? If not, to what relief he is en- 
titled and fiom what date ? 

Whether supersession of Shri T. T. Acharjee of the 
Kathara Washery by Sarvashri B. B. Lai of 
Kargali Colliery and K. P. Singh of Kathara Col- 
liery in the matter of promotion to the post of 
Foreman-in-charge is justified 7 If not, to what 
relief is he entitled and from what date 7" 

After receipt of the above order of reference both sides 
appeared through their authorised representatives and sub 
mitted written statements. Some documents were also filed 
by both sides. The case proceeded along its course and the 
same was fixed for evidence and arguments in due course. 
On 2-5-1974 a memorandum of settlement was filed in this 
court and on 9-5-1974 the contents of the memorandum of 
settlement were verified as coriect by Shri S. S. Mukherjee, 
Advocate representing the employers and by Shri J. D. Lai, 
Advocate representing the workmen, I heard both i sides 
on the terms of settlement resolving the dispute and it was 
submitted before me that as the dispute has been amicably 
settled the terms may be acepted and an award passed 
accordingly. Going through the memorandum of settlement 
I find that the same has been signed by Shri B. Guha, Zonul 
Secretary, National Coal Organisation Employees Associa- 
tion, Kathara, and by Shri J. D. Lai, Advocate, representing 
the workmen and also by the concerned workmen from the 
side of the workmen. From the side of the management 
the same has been signed by Shri S. S. Mukherjee, Advocate, 
I have gone through the terms embodied in the memoran- 
dum of settlement and find them beneficial to both sides. 
There is no reason why the terms of the settlement should 
not be accepted. Accordingly, I make an award in terms 
of th ; memorandum of settlement filed before me which 
do foi m a part of the award as Annexure A. 

ANNEXURE A 

BEFORE THF PRESIDING OFFICER CENTRAL

Coal Development Corporation, 

AND 

Their Workmen— as represented by the NCOEA. 

The paitics above named beg to submit that, without 
prejudice to the contentions contained in their respective 
written statement the disputes in the above Reference has 
been amicably settled on the following 
Terms : — 

(1) Shri T. T, Acharjee of the Kathare Washery will 
be deemed to have been promoted to the post of 
Foreman In-charge w.e.f. 12-9-1969. 

(2) That the above promotion will be considered as a 
national and he will not be entitled to claim any 
arrear payment on account of this promotion. 

(3) That Sri T. T. Acharjee will belong to the Washery 
Cadre and his future claim for seniority, promotion 
etc. will be confined to the Washery Cadre only. 

(4) That by 1-6-1974 Sri T. T. Acharjee will be consi- 
dered for selection and consequent promotion to 
the post of Asstt. Engineer in the Washery Cadre 
according to the existing Rules of N.C.D.C. Ltd. 

(5) That the parties specifically agree that the national 
promotion of Sri T. T. Acharjee as mentioned in 
the terms of the settlement has been made on a 
special consideration and will not be quoted as a 
precedent in other cases whatsoever. 

(6) That since Sri Jainarayan Singh, one of the work- 
man concerned in the reference has since been 
promoted by the management to the post of work 
Supervisor, the Union withdraws the dispute relat- 
ing to Sri Singh from the tribunal on the under- 
standing that it would explore the possibility of 
directly negotiating it with the management so far 
as the question of his seniority is concerned. 

(7) That the above terms of settlement finally resolves 
the dispute pending before the Hon'able Tribunal 
concerning Sri T. T. Acharjee and Sri Jainarayan 
Singh. 

It is, therefore, humbly piayed that the above terms of 
settlement may kindly he accepted and an Award passed in 
terms thereof.

AWARD 

The Government of India, Ministry of Lnbout and Re* 
habihtation in the Department of Labour & Employment 
is of opinion that an industrial dispute exists between the 
employers in relation to the management of Shn Lakhimata 
Colheiy of Messis J k Gorai and Company Post office 
Chirkunda, Distuct Dhanbad and their woikmen Accord- 
ingly, the said dispute has been lelerred to this Tribunal u/s 
10(1 )(d) of the ID Act, 1947 by ordei No L/2012/144/ 
72-1 R II dHtcd 20th January, 1973 for adjudication upon 
the issue as mentioned in the schedule below 

SCHEDULE 

“Whether the action of the management of Srce Lakht 
mata Colliery of Messis J K Gorai and Company, 
Post office Chirkunda, District Dhanbad in laying 
off of the workmen with effect from the 7th Octo- 
ber, 1972 vide then notice dated 7th October, 1972 
is justified l If not to what relief the workmen 
are entitled /” 

On receipt of the above oidei of reference notices weie 
issued to and duly served upon Ihc parties The woikmen 
rcpiesentcd by their union submitted their written statement 
in due couise and also documents The management of 
Sree I akhimatn colliery never appeared inspite of the service 
of notice upon them At one stage the workmen represented 
by the union submitted a petition for impleading Coal Mines 
Authority as a party to the dispute but subsequently it 
appears that the said petition was not pressed The refe- 
rence proceeded along its couise and the employers all along 
remained absent without taking any steps In view of the 
above circumstances the case was taken up for cx-parte 
hearing and the woikmen went it alone 

1 he case of the workmen is that they weie woikmen in 
Lakhimata colliery und the management of tho said colliciy 
was adopting unfau laboui practice by not paying proper 
wages to them and accordingly they formed a branch of 
Bihar Colliery Kamgarh Union in which they weie members 
in their colliery Thereaftet they presented a chaiger of 
demands to the employers thiough their union 'the manage- 
ment sharply leaded to this and laid off the entire strength 
of workmen with a view to victimise them The manage- 
ment of the colheiy has since been taken over by (he Coal 
Mines Authoi ity wef 30-1 1973 The lay off is alleged 
to he illegal and unjustified jesoitcd to by the management 
to victimise the workmen 

By the tcims of reference the point closed is that theic 
was a lay off of workmen in the above colheiy wet 7-10 
1972 and the point open is if such lay off is justified and 


JULY 13, 1974/AsADHA 22, 1806 1761 


what rebel flows in the case of lay off being not justffied. 
The woikmen led exparte evidence in this case examining 
Shu Kcdai Pandey who claims to be the Secretary of the 
Bihar Colliery Kamgaih Union and one Shri Monilal Pal 
who claims to be a workman in the colliery The evidence 
of the Secretary of the union is that the entire workmen of 
the colliery became membeis of Bihar Colheiy Kamgarh 
union He fuither says that the colliery was depriving the 
woikmen of their dues and accordingly a charter of demands 
was placed before the management and the management 
laid off the workmen to haiass them because of their trade 
union activities Shn Monilal Pal, W W. 2 who is a work- 
man in the colliery says that tho Bihar Colliery Kamgarh 
union placed a chartei of demands to the management and 
the management laid off the entire staff His further evi- 
dence is that the entire workmen of the colliery lematned 
laid off npto 3 months after the take over This is all the 
evidence Now u/s 2(kkk) of the ID. Act lay oft means, 
among other things, the failure or inability on the part of 
the employer on account of shortage of law materials or 
breakdown of machinery etc to give employment to a work- 
man whose name is borne on the muster rolls of his indus- 
trial establishment Generally, if the requirements of the 
definition u/s 2(kkk) are satisfied the lay off can be said 
lo be justified If the lay off is malafkle or if the lay oft 
has been declaied in order to victimise the workmen or for 
some othci ulteiior purpose and the requirements of the 
delmilion of lay oft are not fulfilled, the lay off in such cir- 
cumstances can be said to be unjustified The case of the 
woikmen is that they lay off m the present case is unjustified 
inasmuch as it was resorted to victimise the workmen for 
then joining a bade union and placing a charter of demands 
before the company As the workmen claim the lay off 
to be unjustified and as the case is going ex-parte, it has to 
be satisfactorily established that the lay off as declared by 
the colliery was not justified Nothing is forthcoming before 
me to deduce that the requirements of S 2(kkk) were not 
fulfilled befoic declaring the lay off Facts and circum- 
stances arc not also strong enough for me to hold that the 
lay off as dcelaipd by the colheiy was malafide or it was 
revolted lo to victimise the workmen or with some ulterior 
purpose The fact temains that since the inception of the 
lay oft the colliciy never resumed working by calling off the 
lay off It furthei appears liom the evidence tEat the colliery 
again became active after the take over It cannot be said 
with any decree of ceitainty that the colliery will virtually 
close their business only to victimise the workmen I am 
therefore unable to hold that the lay off was unjustified or 
mallide On the other hand, I am to hold in the facts and 
circumstances of the case that the lay off as declared by 
the colliery is justified Now, if the lay off was malafide or 
unjustified then it would not be a lay off sanctioned under 
S 2(kkk) and the relief provided to the laid off workmen 
u/s 25(c) would not be the only relief to which they are 
entitled In that case something more than the statutory 
compensation may be called foi If on the other hand the 
lay off is justified and it satisfies the requirements of the 
definition the only lclief to which the laid off woikmen are 
entitled to is the statutory relief provided under S 25(C). 
Hcie m this case T find that the lay off is justified No 
materials have howevci been placed before me to show far 
all the laid off woikmen are qualified for compensation u/s 
25(C) of the ID Act 

Tn the rtsult the woikmen who have been laid off aie 
entitled to recovci lay off compensation in accordance with 
the piovisions of this Act S 25(C) provides the require- 
ments and limitations as to the entitlement of lay off com- 
pensation The employers in relation to the management of 
Shiee Lakhimata colheiv is therefoie to prepare a list of 
the workmen qualified to get lay off compensation u/s 25(C) 
as cm ly as possible and such woikmeti as found so qualified 
will be entitled to lecover lay off compensation in accord- 
ance with the piovisions of the Industrial Disputes Act : 

This is my award

1762 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JOLT 13, 1974/ASADHA 22, 1896 


Tribunal, Cential Chandigarh, in Ihe industrial dispute bet- 
ween the employers in relation to the Universal Fire nnd 
General Insurance Company Ltd. Amritsar and their work- 
men, which was received by the Central Government on the 
2/th June. 1974. 

BEFORE SHRI H. R. SODHI, PRESIDING OFFICER, 
INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, CENTRAL, CFLANDIGARH 

Reference No. 13/C of 1973 
BETWEEN 

the workman and management of the Universal Fire 
and General Insurance Company, Delhi. 

APPEARANCES : 

Shti R. K. Mittal, — for the workman. 

Survshri P. P. Khurunu and C. L. Naiula — foi the 
management. 

AWARD 

This Reference relates to a dispute raised between the 
employers in relation to the Universal Fire & General Insu- 
rance Company Ltd., referred to hereinafter as the Company, 
and their workman. As a matter of fact, this company, aftci 
the nationalisation of fire and general insurance in India has 
now ceased to function and is merged in United India Fire 
& General Insurance Co. Ltd., which is one of the main 
groups of erstwhile fire and general insmance companies 
nationalised by the Central Government w.e.f. 1-1-74 
though earlier to that only a limited control over the mana- 
gements of such companies had started on 13-5-71 through 
an ordinance. Shri K. L. Gupta, the concerned workman in 
this case whose cause had been espoused by General Insu- 
rance Employees Association, Northern Zone, New Delhi 
was appointed as an Accountant in Amritsar Brunch of 
the company by an order dated 14-12-1962, Ext. R/6, on a 
total remuneration of Rs. 160/- P.M. including dearness 
allowance. The appointment came in the wuke of an appli- 
cation dated 3rd December, 1962 made by the workman. 
The probationary period of the workman was fixed at six 
months as per company’s rules. Shii A. R. Gupta, R. W. 2, 
was the Branch Manager Amritsar at the time of initial 
appointment of the workman and it is deposed by him that 
sanction for the appointment of the workman as Accountant 
might have been received from the Head Office. One Shti 
P. C, Sharma was already working as a clerk-cum-typist 
at the time Shri K. L. Gupta was appointed as Accountant 
and drawing less emoluments. The workmen of the Company 
served a charter of demands on the management 
which led to Reference No. CGIT-2/13 of 1968 to 
the Industrial Tribunal No. 1, Bombay under Section 10(2) 
of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, described hereinafter 
as the Act. One of the demands was for le-classificution of 
the employees of the Company and fixation of their scales 
of pay in terms of the demands. The Tribunal by its award 
published in the Gazette of India dated January 3, 1970, 
refused to re-classify the employees but directed that the 
then existing classification as it prevailed at the Head Office 
of the Company be introduced in all its branches. Para IS 
of the award, extract whereof is Ext, A/2, reads as 
under : — 

“18. I am, however, of the view that the classification 
of various employees prevailing in the Head Office 
at Bombay should be made uniform in all the 
branches of the company at all places all over 
India. In the end I rclcct the demand No. 1 for 
re-classification of employees but however pass the 
following order : — - 

ORDER 

The existing classification of employees at the Head 
Office of the company in Bombay should be intro- 
duced in all the branches of the company at all 
places.” 

In pain 114 of the award are given the pav scales of dif- 
feicnt categories of employees as is indicated in Fxt. A/2. 
Clerks and typists are placed in the pay scale of Rs. 125- 
8- 1 73- 1 0-23 3-1 2-293 -EB- 15-3 68- 17-3 85 (23 years) whereas 


Assistant Cashiers and Cashieis have higher pay scales. Ihe 
Company classified the respondent workman as a clerk and 
gave him the pay scale fixed for that calegoiy of employees. 
He was paid anears accordingly as his earlier 

pay scale was less and he accepted the same with- 

out demur. He, however, subsequently taised an industrial 
dispute that since he was working as a cashier he was entit- 
led to be placed in the higher category. Conciliation procee- 
dings held by the Assistant Labour Commissioner failed as is 
clear from the minutes of the meeting. Ext A/1, as held 
between the parties. The Cential Government then in 
exercise of the powers conferred on it b\ Section 7A and 
clause (d) of sub-section (1) of section 10 of the Indus- 
trial Disputes Act, 1947, rcfcricJ to this Tribunal, the fol- 
lowing matter for adjudication : — 

“Whether the action of the management of Universal 
Eire and Ocneral Insurance Company in placing 
Shri K. L. Gupta, Accountant, Universal Fire and 
General Insurance Company, Amritsar, in the grade 
of Rs. 125-385 admissible to clerk and typists was 
justified? If not, to what relief is he eniitled?" 


Claim statement was filed by the workman and their 
main pica is that Shri K. L. Gupta ought to have been 
classified as Senior Assistant in view of the duties perfor- 
med, by him, moic so, when employees performing same 
or similar duties in Head Office they arc classified as such 
and placed in higher pay scales, it is also stated in the 
claim statement that the concerned workman submitted 
several representations to the management seeking necessary 
relief but nothing came out. The United Fire & General 
Insurance Co. Ltd., made an applicalion that since Ifiat 
company in whose name the reference has been made was 
not functioning and its interest vested in the applicant com- 
pany, the latter be impleaded as a party and permitted to 
file a written statement. Written statement was accordingly 
filed. It is pleaded that the workman at the time of his 
appointment was given a consolidated salary of a typist 
clerk but he was not intended to be appointed as a Senior 
Assistant or a Cashier. Amritsar Branch of the Company is 
stated to be a small branch where only two clerks are 
working. It is further pleaded that the business transacted 
there being meagre, no post of a Senior Assistant or a 
Cashier was nccessPty. A rejoinder was filed by the work- 
men in which avennents of the management were refuted. 
On the pleadings of the parties, the following issues were 
framed : — 


Preliminary ihme 

Whether the Tiibunol has no jurisdiction to adjudicate 
upon the reference for the grounds stated in paras 
(1) and (2) of the written statement, wherein it 
is pleaded inter alia that the subject matter of re- 
ference is not within the competence of this Tri- 
bunal, nor is the respondent management an 
employer of the allegedly aggrieved employee, 
Shri K. L. Gupta ? 

On merits, 


(1) Whether the respondent management was justified in 

placing Shi i K, I . Gupta, AccounLant, posted at 
Amritsar, in the grade of Rs. 125-385 as is admi- 
ssible to clerks and typists ? 

(2) If issue No. (1) is found against the management 
and for the workman, to what relief is tho latter 
entitled ? 


The burden of the preliminary issue and issues on merits 
lay on the management w-liich led evidence first and pro- 
duced Shri G. D. Nayar, Officer Incharge, United India 
Fire & General Insurance Co. Ltd. (Unit: Univetsal), 24, 
Bbagat Ram Rond, Darya Ganj, Delhi R.W.I, and Shri A.R. 
Gupta, Branch Manager, United India Fire & General Insu- 
rance Co. Ltd. posled at Amritsar as RAV.2, No evidence 
was led on the prcliminarv issue nor was the same pressed 
at the time or arguments. The prcliminarv issue thus stands 
decided against the management as not being pressed. 



1763 


: JULY 13, 19 74/AS ADHA 22, 1896 


Sec- 3 ( ii) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA 


The sole question that arises on mcrils is as to what 
duties and responsibilities were assigned to Shri k I . Gupta 
and that in view of his job in what category of employees 
he is entitled to be placed in the light of the al’otesaid 
award. There is no mnnnci of doubt that in his letter of 
appointment, Ext. R/6, the workman has been designated 
as an Accountant. I have also no doubt in my mind lhat 
his appointment was appro\cd by the Head Office and it was, 
inded, admitted by Shri G. D. Nayar R,W. 1, in his state- 
ment. Tt is true that only two clerks were working in the 
office at Amritsar but the company for reasons best known 
to it gave Shri Gupta, higher emoluments and designated 
him as an Accountant in contra distinction to a mere clerk. 
The evidence of Shri A.R. Gupta, AAV.2, who has been in 
the picture throughout inasmuch as he was the Buinch 
Manager when the workman was originally appointed and 
he is still continuing there after the nationalisation of 
general insurance is a pointci to tell us about the nature of 
duties performed by the workman. Amongst the duties 
assigned to the workman aic the maintenance of cash book, 
general lcdgci, receipt of cash and making payments, 
signing of cover notes in absence of the Branch Manager. 
I am satisfied that the workman was being treated by the 
respondent as one occupying a higher status than Shii 
Sharma and the duties assigned to him were also such which 
required greater responsibility. At any rate he was admitte- 
dly working as a cashier though may be that no post of a 
cashier as such had been created in the office at Amritsar. 
Shri G.D. Nayar, R.W. 1, had been working as Accountant 
in the Divisional office at Delhi for sometime. He has stuted 
that posts of Accountants existed only in Head Office or in 
Divisional offices but none in the Branch offices. These Ac- 
countants fall in the category of officers and accounts in 
branch offices, according to the witness are maintained by the 
clerks. On behalf of the management it is vehemently con- 
tended that in the absence of a post existing in the Branch 
office, the claim of the workman that he should be graded 
as a cashier or a senior assistant, both posts being equiva- 
lent, is without any substance. The management loses sight 
of the fact that the Bombay Tribunal has by its award re- 
ferred to above called upon the management that classifica- 
tion of various employees as it prevails in the Head Office 
should be introduced in all branches as well though the de- 
mand for rc-classification was rejected. In para 1 14 of the 
award, scales of pay of different classes of employees are 
stated and they have been made applicable in all the branches 
of the respondent Company. Clerks and typists have been 
placed in one category whcicas Junior Assistants, Assistant 
Cashiers and Stenographers in the other. Cashier and 
Senior Assistants form a still higher class of employees. The 
management was bound to have classified the employees in 
their branches according to the classification as stated in 
para 114 and give them the scales of pay as stated therein, 
The classification has to be job-wise and the grievance of the 
workman that he is not being classified according to the 
award seems to me to be well founded, He is beyond dis- 
pute performing the duties of a cashier though may not be 
entitled to be classified as an Accountant which is the desig- 
nation of the officers woiking in Divisional offices or in Head 
Office. The rationalisation of services of the respondent 
company requires that it should in terms of the award and 
according to the duties assigned to its workmen classify them 


and the mere fact that number of office hands working in 
a Branch is not large does not warrant lhat classification 
should be withheld. I cannot decide whether the work- 
man should be categorised as a Junior Assistant, Assistant 
Cashier or Cashier and Senior Accountant. The manage- 
ment has to adopt one uniform pattern foi all its branches 
and 1 do not, therefore, propose to create difficulties for them 
by declaring this workman to be entitled to be placed in a 
paiticular category. I, however, direct that he should be 
classified in either of the categories (e) and (f), namely, of 
Junior Assistants, Assistant Cashier, and Cashier and Senior 
Assistant according as the nature of the job demands and 
after bearing in mind the pattern which the company is go- 
ing to adopt in all its Blanches. The Reference thus stands 
disposed of accordingly with no order as to costs. 

H. R. SODHI, Presiding Officer

ORDER 

New Delhi the 29th June, 1974 

S.O. 1754. — WHEREAS an industrial dispute exists bet- 
ween the employers in relation to the management of Mor- 
mugao Port Trust, Mormugao and their workmen as repre- 
sented by the Mormugao Port and Railway Workers’ Union, 
Mormugao ; 

AND WHEREAS, the said employers and their workmen 
have by a written agreement under sub-section (11 of sec- 
lion 10 V of the Industiial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), 
agreed to icfcr the said dispute to arbitration and have 
forwarded to the Central Government under sub-section 
(3) of section 10A of the said Act, a copy of the said arbi- 
tration agreement ; 

NOW, THbREEORE, in pursuance of sub-section (3) 
of section 10A of the said Act, the Central Government 
hereby publishes the said agreement. 

AGREEMENT UNDER SECTION 10A OF THE INDUS- 
TRIAL DISPUTES ACT, 1947. 

Between 

Presenting employers. — The Board ol Trustees of the 
Poit of Mormugao. Represented by Sbri R. T. 
Majithia, Chairman. 

Representing Workmen. — Mormugao Port & Railway 

Workers’ Union, Represented by : Shri Miguel 
Alfonsi, President. 

It is hcieby agiccd between the parties to refer the fol- 
lowing industrial dispute to the arbitration of Sbri T.S. San- 
karan, Toint Secretary, Ministry of Labour, Sliram-Shakti 
Bhavan, Rali Marg, New Delhi, 

(i) Specific mattcis in dispute. — In the context of re- 
pot t of the Ccntial Wage Board for Port and Dock 
Workeis, the decisions of the Government thereon, 
and other related matters, the demands raised by the 
All India Port and Dock Workers Federation and 
the further discussions held on these, the follow- 
ing matters in dispute [dating to Port and Dock 
Workers of the Major Ports are agreed to be re- 



Sec. 3 (ii) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : 


fcrrcd to arbitration under Section IOA of the In- 
dustrial Disputes Act, 1 747, for decision on merits. 

(1) Whether, and, if so, to what extent the rates for 
recovery of rent for standard houses proposed by 
Government, namely, 7 l|2 per cent of basic pay 
(and not City Compensatory allowance), where 
basic pay is less than Rs. 200 per mensem, and 
at the rote of 1(1 per cent of basic pay (and nol 
City Compensatory Allowance), if it is Rs. 200 
per mensem or more, should be reduced taking into 
account the subsidy clement in the Subsidised 
Industrial Housing Scheme and other relevant 
factors. 

(2) Whether in the matter of fixation of pay in the 
revised scales accepted by the Government on 
the basis of the Central Wage Board Report for 
Port and Dock Workers at Major Poits, the interim 
Relief of Rs. 11.80 per mensem or part thereof 
granted by Government as recommended by the 
Wage Board should be taken into account. 

(3) Whether Dearness Allowance (including additio- 
nal Dearness Allowance and increases in Dearness 
Allowance from time to time) in part or full 
should be treated as pay for the purpose of House 
Rent Allowance and City Compensatory Allow- 
ance. 

(ii) Details of parlies to the dispute including the names 
and addresses of (he establishments or undertakings 
involved : 

(1) The Board of Trustees of the Port of Mormu- 

gao, Mormuguo— Goa. 

(2) Mornmgao Port & Railway Workers' Union, Vasco 
da Gama — Goa, 

(iii) Name of the Unions: Mormugno Port & Railway 
Workers Union. 

(iv) Total number of workmen employed in the under- 
taking affected : 2247, 

(v) Estimated number of workmen affected or likely to 
be affected by the dispute : 2247. 

The arbitrator shall make his award within a period of 
three months or within sucli further time as is extended hv 
mutual agreement between us in writing. 

The previous agreement entered into bv us on 22-6-1971 
and published by the Government of India, Ministry of 
Labour and Rehabilitation (Department of Labour and Emp- 
loyment) in Part II. Section 3, sub-section (ii) of tb' 
Gazette of India Vide their Ol der No. L-390 1 3 [ I J73-P&D 
(viii) dated 17-7-1973, to refer the above mentioned industrial 
dispute to the arbitration of Shri A. T. Zambre, Presiding 
Officer, Central Government Industrial Tribunal-cum-Laboii'- 
Court, No. 2, 4th Floor, City Ice Building, 298, Raznrgnle 
Street, Fort, Bombay is hereby cancelled.

report of the Central Wage Board for Port and Dock Wor- 
kers, the decisions of the Government thereon and other 
related matters, the demands raised by the All India Port 
and Dock Workers Federation and the further discussions

Port and Dock Workers of the Major Ports are agreed to be 
relerred to arbtartion under Section 10A of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947, for decision on merits : — 

(1) Whether, and, if so, to what extent, the rates for 
recovery of rent for standard houses proposed by 
Government, namely, 7-1/2 per cent of basic pay 
(and not City Compensatory allowance), where 
basic pay is less than Rs, 200 per mensem, and at 
the late of 10 per cent of basic pav (and not City 
Compensatory Allowance), of it is Rs. 200 per 
mensem or more, should be reduced taking into ac- 
count the subsidy element in the Subsidised Indust- 
rial Housing Scheme and other relevant factors.

ORDER 

S.O. 1756.— Whereas an industrial dispute exists bet- 
ween the employers in relation to the management of United 
Slevedores Association of Cochin ( Private J Limited, Cochin-1 
and their workmen as represented by Cochin Thurumugha 
Thozhilali Union and Steamer Tally Clerks’ Association, 
Cochin-2 ; 

And, whereas, the said employers and their workmen 
have by a written agreement undei sub-section fl) of sec- 
tion 10A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1047 (14 of 1947), 
agreed to refer the said dispute to arbitiution and have for- 
warded to the Central Government undei sub-section (3) 
of section 10A of the said Act, a copy of the said arbitra- 
tion agreement ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of sub-section (3) of section 
10A of the said Act, the Central Government heieby pub- 
lishes the said agreement.

ORDER 

S.O, 1760. — Wheieas an industrial dispute exists between 
the employers in relation to the management of Bombay 
Stevedores’ 'Association Limited, Bombay and their workmen 
us rcpiescnted by Transport and Dock Workers’ Union, 
Bombay ; 

And, whereas, the said employers and the said union have, 
by a written agieenrent in pursuance of the provisions of sub- 
section (! 1 ) of section 10A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 
1947 (14 of 1947), agreed to refer the said dispute to 
urbitiation ol the person mentioned theiein, and a copy of 
the said atbitration agreement has been forwarded to the 
Central Government and the same has been published, under 
the provisions of sub-scction (3) of the said section with the 
order of the Government of India in the Ministry of Labour 
No. L-39013/1 /73-P&D (V) dated the 1st July, 1974 
published in Part II. Section 3, sub-section (ii) of the 
Gazelle of India, dated the 13th July, 1974; 

And, whereas, the Central Goveinmnet is satisfied that 
the persons making the said reference represent the majority 
of the party ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of the provisions of sub- 
section (3A) of the said section, read with rule 8A of the 
Industrial Disputes (Central Rules), 1957, the Central 
Government hereby notifies for the information of the em- 
ployer’s and workmen who arc not parties to the said arbitra- 
tion agreement but who are concerned with the said dispute 
that the persons making the said reference represented the 
majority of each party.

Raft Marg, New Delhi. 

(i) Specific matters in dispute. — In the context of teport 
of the Central Wage Board for Port and Dock 
Workets, the decisions of the Government thereon, 
and other related matters, the demands raised by 
the All India Pot t and Dock Workers’ Federation 
and the futther discussions held on these, the 
following matters in dispute relating to Port and 
Dock Workets of the Major Ports tiro agreed to be 
referred to mbitiation under Section 10A of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, for decision on 
mfrits : — 

(1) Whether, and, if so to what extent, the lates 
lor recovery, of rent for standard houses pur- 
posed by Government, namely, 74- percent 
of basic pay (and not City Compensatory Allowance 
where basic pay is less than Rs, 200/- per tnen- 
sum and at the rate of 10 per cent of basic pay 
(and not City Compensator Allowance), if it is 
Rs. 200/- per mansnm or more, should be 
reduced taking into account the subsidy element 
in the Subsidised Industrial Housing Scheme and 
other relevant factors. 

(2) Whether in the matter of fixation of pay in Ihe 
revised scales accepted hy the government on the 
basis of the Central Wage Board Repoit for 
Port and Dock Workers at Major Ports, the 
inteitm relief of Rs. 11.80 Per Mensem or part 
thereof granted by government as recommended 
by the Wage Board should be taken into account. 

(3) Whether Dearness Allowance (including addi- 
tional Dcurness Allowance and increases in Deai- 
ness Allowance from time to time) in part or 
full should be treated as pay for the purpose of 
House Rent Allowance and City Comprnsafoiy 
Allowance. 

tii) Details of parties to (he dispute including the 
names and addresses of the establishments or undei- 
takings involved : 

(1) Chipping & Painting Ftn clovers’ Association 

Private Ltd., 3rd (loor, lanmabhoomi Chambers,

liii) Name of the Union: Transport Sc Dock Worker*

taking affected : Approximately 1,000. 

( \ ) Estimated number of workmen affected or likely 
to be affected by the dispute : Approximately 1,000.

ORDER 

S.O. 1764. — Whereas an industi ial dispute exists between 
the employers in relation to the management of Bombay Port 
Dust Bombay and their workmen as represented by Bombay 
Pent Tnist Employees’ Union, Bombay; 

And, whereas, the said employers and their workmen hnve 
by .1 wiitling agiet-ment under sub-section (1) of section 10A 
of the Industii.d Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1447), agreed to 
icier the said dispute to aibitration and have forwarded to 
the Central Government, under sub-section (3) of section 
10A of (he said Act, a copy of the said arbitration 
agreement. 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of sub-section (3 ) of section 
10A of the said Act, the Central Government hereby pub- 
lishes Lhe said agreement. 

'AGREEMENT UNDER SECTION 10A OE THF 
INDUSTRIAL DISPUTES ACT, 1947. 

BETWFEN 

Representing employers : BOMBAY PORT TRUST, BOMBAY 
Representing Workmen : B-P.T. Employees’ Union, Bombay 

It is hereby agreed between the parties to refer the follow- 
ing industrial dispule to the arbitration of Shri T, S. Sankarn, 
Joint Secretary, Ministry of Labour, Sbram Shakti Bhavan, 
Raft Marg, New Delhi. 

(i) Specific matters In dispute. — In the context of report 

of the Central Wage Board for Port and Dock 
Workers, the decisions of the Government thereon, 
and other related matters, the demands raised by 
the All India Port and Dock Workers’ Federation 
and the further discussions held on these, the 
following matters in dispute relating to Port and 
Dock Workers of the Major Ports are agreed to be 
referred to aibitration under Section lOA^of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, for decision on 
merits : — 

( 1 ) Whether, and, if so to what extent, the rates 
for recovery of rent for standard houses pro- 
posed by Government, namely, 7-1/2 per cent 
of basic pay (and not City Compensatory 
Allowance), where basic pay is less than Rs. 200 /- 
per mensem and at the rate of 10 per cent of 
basic pay (and not City Compensatory Allowance), 
if it is Rs. 200 per mensem or more, should be 
reduced taking into account the subsidy element 
in the Subsidised Industrial Housing Scheme and 
other relevant factors. 

(2; Whether in the matter of fixation of pay in the 
revised scales accepted by the govei ument on the 
basis of the Central Wage Board Report for 
Port and Dock Workers at Major Ports, the 
.intcrirti relief of Rs, 11,80 Per Mensem or part 
thereof granted by government as recommended 
by the Wage Board should be taken into account 

(3) Whether Dearness Allowance (including addi- 
tional Dearness Allowance and increases in Dear- 
ness Allowance from time to time! in part or 
full should be treated as pay for the purpose of 
House Rent Mlowance and City Compensatory 
Allowance. 

(ii) Details of parties to the dispute including the 
names and addresses of the establishments or under 
takings involved ; 

(1) BOMBAY PORT TRUST Shoorji Vallabhdas 
Marg, Fort, Bombay-400038. 

(2) B.P.T, Employees’ Union, Kamgar Sadan. Nawab 
'lank Ovci bridge, Mazagaon, Bombay 400010. 

(ili) Name of the union : B. P. T. Employees Union, 
Bombay. 

(iv) Total number of workmen employed in the under- 

taking affected — 29,800 (Approximately), 

(v) Estimated number of workmen affected or likely to 

be affected by the dispute— 29,800.

S.O. 1765.— Whereas an industrial dispute exists between 
the employers in relation to the management of Bombay Port 
Trust, Bombay and their workmen as represented by Trans- 
port and Dock Workers' Union, Bombay; 

And, whereas, the said employers and their workmen have 
by a written agreement under sub-section (1) of section 10A 
of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), agreed to 
refer the said dispute to arbitration and have forwarded to 
the Central Government, under sub-section (3) of section 
10A of the said Act, a copy of the said arbitration 
agreement. 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of sub-section (3) of section 
10A ol the said Act, the Central Government hereby pub- 
lishes the said agreement. 

AGREEMENT UNDFR SECTION 10A OP 
THE INDUSTRIAL DISPUTES ACT, 1947 

BETWEEN 

Representing employers: Bombay Port Trust, Bombay. 

Representing Workmen: Transport and Dock Workers’ 
Union, Bombay. 

It is hereby agieed between the parties to refer the follow- 
ing industrial dispute to the arbiration of Shri T. S. Sanknrn,

Rail Marg, New Delhi. 

(i) Specify mallei s in dispute . — In the context of rcpoit 
of the Central Wage Board foi Port and Dock 
Woikeis, the decisions of the Government thereon. 


and olhei 1 elated mallei s, the demands raised by 
the All India Poil and Dock Workers’ Pederation 
and the further discussions held on these, the 
following matters in dispute relating to Port and 
Dock Workers of the Major Ports are agreed to be 
referred to arbitration under Section 10A of the 
Industiiul Disputes Act. 1947, for decision on 
merits : — 

(1 ) Whether, and, il so to what exlept, the rates 
for recovery of tent for standard houses pro- 
posed bv Government, namely, 7-1/2 per cent 
of basic pay (and not City Compensatory 
Allowance), vheie basic pay is less than Rs. 200/- 
per mensem and at the rate of 10 per cent of 
basic pay (and not City Compensatory Allowance), 
if il is Rs. 200 per mensem or mote, should be 
1 educed taking into account the subsidy element 
in the Subsidised Industrial Housing Scheme and 
other relevant factors, 

(2) Whether in the matter of fixation of pay in the 

revised scales accepted by the government on the 
basis of the Central Wage Board Report f 01 
Port and Dock Workers at Major Ports, the 

interim relief of Rs. 11.80 per mensem or port 
thereof granted by government as recommended 
by the Wage Board should be taken into account. 

(3) Whether Dearness Allowance (including addi- 
tional Dearness Allowance and incieases in Dear-

4. Ihe General Secretary, Goa Dock Labour Union has

men. 

5. According to the General Sccrctaty of the Ooa Dock 
Labour Union:— 

(i) The company is a paitnership firm ca irving on the 
business of Stevedoring and I aunehing operation 
in the Port of Mormugao, The company is doing 
excellent business. 

lii) On 31-12-1968 the company issued leticnchment 
notices to the members of the staff including Ihe 
launch uevv informing (hem that the firm was not 
doing sufficient business to warrant ihe mainten- 
ance of the present office staff and thereloie the 
company was compelled to retrench the same with 
effect from 31-12-1968. To the launch ciew the 
company informed that since the motoi launches 
were i mining into iccuning losses during the last 
live yeats the company intending laying them up 
with effect fiom 31-12-1 968 and hence the ser- 



1786 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JULY 13, 1974/ASADHA 22, 1896 Part II— 


vices of the launch ctevv weie no longer required. 
These notices ol retrenchment were seised upon the 
workers who aie covered by this Reference nnd 
such notices were not served upon those whom the 
company wanted to retain in its sen ices. While 
selecting the workers for retrenchment the com- 
pany did not select them according to the principle 
of ‘first come last go’. The company did not give 
them retrenchment compensation on 31-12-1968 Le- 
an the day on which the services weie letrcnched. 

(iii) The ictrenchment notices and the consequent ret- 
renchment are malafide, and illegal. This retrench- 
ment was with a view to victimise the woikeis. 
The reason for termination of services which is 
given us ‘not doing sufficient business’ is absolutely 
false and unreal. The company was not incurring 
losses but it was prospering. 

(iv) Though the company ostensibly claims its dissolu- 
tion, in leality the same partners have t'oimcd a 
new company and the staff which war. engaged in 
this company is being asked to join the new com- 
pany as new employees on much reduced .salary 
and other emoluments. This shows that the real 
reason behind retrenchment was to reduce wages 
and emoluments of the workers. 

(v) The company has not paid retrenchment compen- 

sation but it has paid gratuity to the workers. As 
retrenchment compensation as per the provisions 
of Section 251- (b) was not paid to them, the ret- 
renchment is illegal and void. 

(vi) As the action of lhe company in retrenching this 
employees is illegal, the workmen are entitled to 
reinstatement in servico of the company on pay- 
ment of full wages lor the period of their foieed 
unemployment. 


6. According to the company: — 


(l) It has ceused doing the business of Ste\ edoiing and 
launching with effect fiom 31-12-1968 as it closed 
down its business with effect from the said date. 
Its Balance-sheet and Profit and Loss Account for 
the year 1966 showed a meagre profit of Ks, 223.14. 
Its Balance-sheet and Profit and Loss Account ioi 
the years 1967 and 1968 showed a loss of Rs. 
68,357.53 and Rs, 39,115.79 respectively. It suffer- 
ed a total loss of Rs. 1,07,000 completely wiping 
out the capital of the company, which was 

Rs. 1,00,000/-. In these circumstances the part- 
ners of the company decided to discontinue the 
business. Tt was not doing good business. 

(ii) It served retrenchment notices to all the woikmen 
and paid them retrenchment compensation. As the 
services of all the workmen were terminated, the 
question of the company selecting the workmen on 
tne principle of ‘Last come first go’ cannot arise. 

It had closed down its business with effect from 
31-12-1968 and the workmen were offered compen- 
sation on the nsutil pay scale in accordance with 
the provisions of the Act. It has not violated any 
provisions of the law. As the termination was ou 
account of closure, payment of compensation was 
not a condition precedent. 

(iii I As there was no scheme of gratuity, thcie was no 
question ol paying gratuity to the workmen The 
compensation in accordance with the provisions of 
law was paid to the workmen. 

0\) The workmen aie not entitled to lchcl of miss- 
tatement inasmuch as there has been a dissolution 
of partnership, with effect from 31-12-1968, The 
question ol reinstating the woikmen or paying the 
full wages ufter 31-12-1968 cannot niise, 

( v ) The company has no peimnncnt staff' .it picscnt. 
The management also approached various other 


companies, friends and sympathizers with an at- 
tempt to provide employment to the retrenched staff 
concerned in this reference and almost all have by 
now secured employment.

POIN r NO, 1 

13 M/N. limn leitao and Company T td were carrying 
on Stevedonng and launch Transpoil business in Moinuiyao

Harbour. It had two partners viz. Shri Floy Sebustiao Dias 
and Shri lose Xavior Marcelino da Piedade Lima Leitao. 
On 31-12-1968 the assets and liabilities of this him were 
determined and the partner Shri Dias retired lrom this firm 
with effect from 1-1-1969. The Continuing partnei Shri Lima 
Leitao was to continue the business as sole proprietor. JHe 
was at liberty to continue the firm under the name of Messrs 
Lima Leitao A: Co. He was at liberty to admit any other 
pci son or persons as partners in the continuing firm after 
all payments due to Shri Dias were made. These terms 
appear in the document Ex. 80 'E. 

14. Shri Rele appearing on behalf of the company contends 
that as one of the partner Shri Dias retired from the com- 
pany, it stood dissolved automatically and that it ceased to 
continue its business. This contention cannot be accepted. 

15. It is true that one of the partners Shri Dias retited 
from the company with effect from 1-1-1969 but the con- 
tinuing partner carried on the business as sole proprietor. 
It cannot he therefore said that the firm ceased to function 
with etleet from l-J-1969 and that it ceased to do business. 

16. List of Stevedores registered in Mormuguo Dock Labour

was cai i icd on and must not have been closed.

the death of Shri Lima) Shi i and Sint l.imn l eitao. The same

Ltd, This circumstance indicates that the Stevedoring business 
carried on the licence of M/s, Lima l.citao and Co. Pvt. 
Ltd. by M/s. Lima Minerals Pvt. Ltd. is the continuance of 
the business of the company. 

34. Shri Rele for the company contends that circumstance 
that M/s. Lima Minerals Pvt. Ltd. is doing the Stevedoring 
business on the I icence of M/s. lima r eitao & Co. Pvt. Ltd. 
or M/s. Lima l.eitao and Co. Ltd. will not necessarily show

nuing. If M/s. l ima Minerals Pvt. Ltd. is making use of

ent thing. 1 am unable to accept this argument. 

35. The circumstance that all accounting with the Dock 
Labour Board is in the name of tbo company i.e. Messrs. 
Lima Leitao & Co. Ltd. or Messrs. Limn Leitao & Co. Pvt. 

I td. indicates that the business of the company is continuing 
by th > same parties. 

36. Shri Rcle contends : 

(1) no business was done by Messrs, Lima Leitao &

37. As the services of all the workmen were terminated 
their Provident Fund had to be returned. As their services 
were terminated there was no question of continuing the 

rovident fund in their name. As the business is transacted 
y M/s. Lima Minerals Pvt. Ltd. keeping accounts in its

not do any business after 31-12-1968 because he retired. In 
my opinion on the basis of ull the facts referred to above 
by Shri Rcle on the basis of the evidence adduced by the 
company before me, it cannot be said that M/s, Lima Leitao 
and Company l td. had dosed its Stevedoring business on 
31-12-1968. 

38. Shri Sowani for the Union contends that there was 
no real closure of business, that Stevedoring and launch

denting of labour from tbc Mormugao Dock Labour Board

39. From the evidence of Shri Vclhni Ex. 8/W and the

his evidence and Shi i Onknrappu, F.x.-43/W and documents 
produced in his evidence, I find that there was no real closure 
of the business of Stevedoring by M/s. Limn Leitao and Co. 
T td. 


40. The evidence of Shri Morgulhao, Ex. 43/E show- 
ing that the business was closed on account of 
losses incurred in the business is difficult to accept. Smt. 
Urminda Lima Leitao, Ex. 61 /E says that the business was 
closed down but the documents showing the transactions with 
the Dock Labour Board in the name of M/s. Lima Leitao & 
Co. Ltd. indicate that the Stevedoring business was continued. 

41. Shri John Fernandes, Ex. 4/W was working as Tally 
Clerk in the company. According to him, the company has 
got two launches. The company is still operating these two

Sputnik and Lunik are still standing in the name of the com- 
pany. 

45. The company contends that the launch Transport busi- 
ness was also closed.

the work was done.

THE GAZETTE OF INDTA : JULY 13, 1974/ASADHA 22, 1S96 


51. It may be that lunch transport business may be running 
into losses but it cannot be said fiom the evidence on record 
that the launches ceased to operate. They were operating now 
and then. It cannot be thercfoie said that launch transport 
business was closed. 

52. In short, consideiing the evidence of six witness exa- 
mined on behalf of the workman, documentary evidence and 
the four witnesses of the company and the documents pro- 
duced by them alongwith circumstances brought on tecord, 1 
find that there was no real closure of business. Hence my 
finding on point No. i is as above. 

I'oint No. ii 

53. In he present case the company gave notice to all the 
employees mentioned in the schedule and retrenched them 
on the ground that the company was not having any business 
i.c. running into losses. 

54. The company can intrench the employees provided pro- 
visions of Section 25F of the I.D. Act are complied with. 
The evidence of Smt. Urminda Lima Leitao, Ex, 61/E shows 
that on termination of services, compensation and other bene- 
fits were given to each employee, and that each employee 
has been given compensation under Sec. 25EFF read with 
Section 25F. 

55. In the present case, as there was no real closure of 
business, the termination of services of the employees would 
amount to retrenchment under Section 25F of the T.D. Act, 
1947. Hence provisions of Section 25F of the l.D. Act had 
to be complied with, 

56. It appears that in the present case the retrenchment 
compensation was not paid on 31-12-1968 but it was paid 
later on, vide Register of Wages. 

57. If is contended on behalf of the employees that they 
were paid gratuity and not compensation. The company on 
the other hand contends that theie was no scheme under 
which the employees were entitled to gi amity at the time 
of retirement and that no gratuity was paid to them, 

58. Shri Jose Cota, Ex. 56/F. says in his cross-examination 
ns follows : — 

“Witness is shown page marked T by the Tribunal, 
the word compensation has been written by me 
after erasing the word Gratuity. This was done 
under the instiuctions of Shri Morgulhao. The whole 
of the heading is written, erasing the earlier heading, 
I do not recollect what the other erased words 
were there. 

Col. 1 1 the words compensation due month have been 
written after erasing the earlier writings. The word 
compensation has been written after erasing the 
words gratuity. 

I am shown page 6 and the portion marked 
‘X’ on it. 1 did not see any writing on this 
marked portion. It is true that the writing on 
this maiked portion has been erased. This register 
wrs handled by Shri Morgulhao and myself. I have 
not erased the writing in the marked portion ‘X’ 
on pngc No, 6,” 

59. Shri Salvador Morgulhao, Ex. 45/E says in his cross- 
examination as follows : — 

“I am shown the entries in the Register of Wages on 
pages marked 1 to 6. At the time of signing the 
Register, the word compensation for general beading 
and compensation duc/month were there. I say that 
the earlier words have been erased and compensa- 
tion has been written in that place. This was done 
hy my assistant Shii lose Cota under my instruc- 
tions. 

It is tiue that it appears that something was written 
on the portion on page 6 marked ‘X’ but it appears 
to be erased, 1 do not know who erased this por- 
tion.” 


Part II — 


60. According to Shri Morgulhao, Ex. 45 /H there was no 

gratuity scheme in the company from 1957 to 1968, gratuity 
was not paid to any employees during this period. I am 
shown register of wages. There are three pages in the 
icgistcr with heading ‘The compensation paid to employees 
upto 31-12-1968 ’* 

"On 1-1-1969 when T went to Shri Dias he gave me 
a slip of paper mentioning gratuity and he had 
asked me to calculate on the basis of 15 days 
salary per year of service put in by employees for 
which paid to them. Accordingly I worked out the 
amount paid to each employee T asked my Assistant 
Shri Jose Cota to enter it on the register of wages. 
On having done so and after veryfylng by me, I 
placed the book before Shri Lima as per practice 
When he went through the book he told me that 
the heading was wrong and correct heading should 
be compensation. Accordingly I asked my assistant 
to change the heading from gratuity into compen- 
sation on Wage Book. This was done on 3rd or 4th 
Jan. 1969 before payments were actually made.’’ 

61. It appears to me from the evidence of the two witness 
that through some misunderstanding the word gratuity was 
written but later on, the same was corrected as the intention 
was to pay compensation on the basis of 15 days salary per 
year of service. Hence no blame can be attributed to the 
company and its employees for making correction in the 
register erasing the word gratuity and writing the word com- 
pensation. Tf the employees would have been entitled to gra- 
tuity under any provisions of the law or on the basis of 
written agreement, the matter would have been different. I 
therefore hold that compensation was paid to the employees 
and not gratuity. 

62. As the compensation and notice pay were not paid 
on the date of retrenchment, the retrenchment of the em- 
ployees in queston is invalid and not Justified. Hence mv 
finding on point No. ii is as above. 

Point No. iii 

63. As the retrenchment of the employees is not justified 
they are entitled to reinstatement or compensation, 

64. In the present case I find from the facts and circumstan- 
ces on record that the reinstatement would not be proper. 

65. As the employees are not to he given reinstatement, 
they huve to be paid compensation in lieu of reinstatement. 

66. While considering the amount of compensation it is 
necessary to consider some of the important facts in this 
case. 

67. On 23-12-1968 there was settlement between the Union 
on behalf of the employees and the company in respect of 
payment of D.A, and interim relief on the basis of Wage 
Board recommendations. By this settlement bonus was also 
agreed to be paid at the rate of 4 per cent for the years from 
1963 onwards. This settlement was implemented and all the 
payments were made to the employees, 

68. On retrenchment of the employees, notice pay and re- 
trenchment compensation were paid to the employees but 
the same was not paid on 31-12-1968 but later on within 
a few days. Taking all these facts into consideration I am 
of the view that three months wages in lieu of reinstatement 
should be given to the employees mentioned in the Schedule 
except S/shri S. Morgulhao, and Menino Aphonso and those 
who are employed by M/s, Lima Leitao and Co. Pvt. Ltd, 
or M/s. Lima Minerals Pvt. Ltd. 

69. I therefore hold that the employees in this reference 
are entitled to relief as mentioned above. 

Point No. iv 

70. In view of the above findings 1 pass the following 

order: — 

ORDER 

(i) It is hereby declared that the action of the manage- 
ment of Messrs. Lima Leitao and Company Limited,

Vasco-da-Gama in retrenching the workmen men- 
tioned in the Schedule above with effect from 
31-12-1968 and 1-1-1969 is not justified and that each 
employee except Shri S. Mergulhao and Shri Menino 
Aphonso and those who are employed cither in 
M/s. Lima Leitao and Co. Pvt. Ltd. or M/s. Lima 
Minerals Pvt. Ltd. should be given three months 
wages in lieu of reinstatement 

(ii) Award is made accordingly. 

(iii) Parties to bear their own costs.

1792 THE GAZETTE OF 1NDTA : JULY 13, 1974/ASADHA 22, 1896 


The statement of claim was filed by Rashtriya Gypsum 
Karamchari Sangh, An application was lateron filed on 
behalf of Gypsum Mine Workers Union with a inquest to 
implead them us party in the reference. Ibis application 
was accepted and the Union was made a paity. but no 
separate statement of claim was filed an behalf of this 
Union. 

The claim of the Sangh is that the management of the 
Bikaner Gypsum Ltd., Bikaner was absolutely unjustified in 
laying off 14 workmen and further that even though the 
strike was called oft on 21-10-67, the lav-off of these work- 
men continued. 

In reply to this claim the management pleaded that the 
matter of contractor’s workmen was under negotiation bet- 
ween the contractors and their workmen. Conciliation pro- 
ceedings were being held by the Regional Laboui Commis 
sioncr but in the mean time the Gypsum Mine Workers 
Union called on un illegal strike. As a result of this strike 
and obslruction created at the instance of the Union, loading 
came to a virtual standstill and the management suffered a 
great deal on this account. It is further contended lhat us 
the louding was not possible, accumulation of stocks took 
place at Jamsar sidings. Further the mining of Gypsum was 
completely dislocated and normal work was not possible 
and the management had to lay-off the least number of 
workmen. Further that inspitc of the fact that the strike 
was called off with effect fiom 21-10-67 the lcusons for 
lay-off continued. The management was, thercfoie, justi- 
fied in laying off the 14 workmen. 

Tn support of the claim one Dilbag Singh, Vice President 
of the Sangh put in his affidavit. He was cross examined on 
hehalf of the management. 

In the cross examination Dilbagh Singh admitted that the 
material accumulated at the siding due to the stiikc of the 
contractor’s workmen. He further admitted that even after 
the call off the strike huge stock of material continue to 
accumulate. 

In rebuttal the management filed the affidavit of Shri A, 
K. Mukherji, Personnel Manager of the Company but he 
was not cross examined by the Sangh or the Union. 

I have looked into the pleadings and the evidence and 
find that the Sangh and the Union have failed to establish 
their claim. The sole statement of Dilbagh Singh does not 
prove that the action of the management ill laying off 14 
workmen was unjustified or illegal. It may further be 
noted that the Sangh stopped taking interest in this matter 
and did not utlend the hearing since 21-8-1971. In view ol 
these insufficient evidence on record I am not cotnenccd to 
hold that the action of the management in laying oil 14 
workmen was unjustified or illegal. The result is that a no 
dispute award is passed. 

U. N. MATHUR, Presiding Officer. 

Central Govt. Industrial Tribunal, Rajasthan, 

JAIPUR.

BP FORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL 
TRIBUNAL (NO. 2) AT DHANBAD 

PRESENT: 

Shri K. K. Sarkur, Judge, Presiding Officer. 

REFERENCE NO. 4 OF 1974. 

In the matter of an industrial dispute tl/s 10(1) (d) of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 

PARTIES: Employers in relation to the management 
of Eastern Manganese and Minerals Limited, Tost 
Office Domehanch, District Huzaiihaph. 

AND 

Their woAmen. 

APPEARANCES: 

On behalf of the employers: None. 

On behalf of the workmen: None. 

State: Bihar Industry: Manganese. 

Dhanbad, 19th June, 1974, 

AWARD 

The Government of India, Ministiy of Labour is of opi- 
nion that an industrial dispute exists between the manage- 
ment of Eastern Manganese and Minerals Limited Post 
Office Domehanch, District Hazaribagh and their workmen, 
Accordingly ,by their order No. L-27011 (8 ) /73-LRIV dated 
4-1-74 they referred the same to this Tribunal u/s 10(1 )(d) 
of the ID. Act, 1947 for adjudication upon the issue as 
mentioned in the schedule below : 

SCHEDULE 

“Whether the workmen employed by Messrs Eastern 
Manganese and Minerals Limited, Post office Dom- 
chanch, District Hnzaribagh are entitled to bonus 
at the rate of 20 per cent of earned wages during 
the accounting year commencing in 1971 9 If not 
to what quantum of bonus are the workmen en- 
titled for the above mentioned accounting year?’’ 

After receipt of the order of reference notices were duly 
issued to and scived on the parties. Chances were given 
one after another to the parties to file their written state- 
ments in respect of the industrial dispute in question. It 
appears that at the initial stage one Shri P. N. Sarka^ Mem- 
ber, Executive Commiltee, Mctallifcious Mine Workcis As- 
sociation appeared and prayed for time for filing written 
statement. Time was allowed by this Court for tile pur- 
pose. Thereafter both sides failed to appear and lake any 
steps on several occasions when the case was fixed for the 
purpose. On 7-5-74 the Court by its order alerted parties 
that in default of their taking steps as directed by the Court, 
the case will be disposed of according to law on the next 
dated fixed, Still then on the next date fixed foi the pur- 
pose i.e. on 6-6-74 none of the parties appeared and took 
any steps. This continued default on the part of the both 
the management and the workmen leads me to believe that 
they arc no longer interested to prosecute the case as some 
soit of settlement must have been arrived at between them 
out of Court. In view of the above i am led to believe 
that no industrial dispute exists any moie between the pai- 
ties. Accoidingly, this reference is disposed of on a ‘No 
dispute’ award. 


K. K. SARKAK, Presiding Officer,

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 6th June, 1974 

8.0. 1772.— Whereas the Central Government is of opinion 
that an industrial Dispute exists between the employers in 
relation to the United Commercial Bank and their workmen 
in respect of the matter specified in the Schedule hereto 
annexed ; 

And, whereas the Central Government considers it desirable 
to refer the said dispute for adjudication ; 

Now, therefore in exercise of the powers conferred by 
section 7A, and clause (d) of sub-section (1) of section 10, 
of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the 
Central Government hereby constitutes an Industrial Tribunal 
of which Shri Updesh Narain Mathur shall be the Presiding 
Officer, with headquarters at Jaipur and refers the said dispute 
for adjudication to the said Tribunal.

9th June, 1970 to 31st December, 1970 and from 
the 8th February 1971 to 15th March, 1971 is 
justified ? if not to what relief is he entitled in 
terms of the Promotion Policy in force from the 
9th December, 1968 ?”

New Delhi, the 1st July, 1974 

S.O. 1777.— In exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d.) 
of sub-section (1) of section 5 A. of the Employees provi- 
dent funds and Family pension Fund Act, 1952 (19 of 
1952), the Central Government hereby appoints Shrl P. 
Chentsal Rao, Additional Secretary-General, All India Orga- 
nisation of Employers, Federation House, New Dclhi-1 as 
a member of Central Board of Trustees, vice Shri Surrottam 
P. Hutheesing, and makes Ihe following further amendment 
in the notification of the Government of India in the Late 
Ministry of Labour, Employment and Rehabilitation (Depart- 
ment of Labour and Employment) No. S. O. 2412, duted the 
6th July, 1970, namely : — 

In the said notification in serial number 21, for the entry 
“Shi i Surrottam P. Hutheesing, Shnhibag, Ahmedabad", the 
entry “Shri P. Chentsal Rao, Additional Secretary-General, 

All India Organisation of Employers, Federation House, New 
Delhi- 1” shall be substituted. 

[No. 12 (5) /69-P.F, 111 
DAT.TIT SINGH, Secy.

MINISTRY OF SUPPLY AND REHABILITATION 
(Department of Rehabilitation) 

New Delhi, the 29th June, 1974 

S.O. 1778. — In exercise of the powers conferred by Sub- 
Section (I) of Section 3 of the Displaced Persons (Compen- 
sation and Rehabilitation) Act, 1954 (44 of 1954), the 
Central Government hereby appoints the Member of the 
Board of Revenue in the State of Andhra Pradesh dealing 
with evacuee property as Settlement Commissioner for the 
purpose of performing, in addition to his own duties as 
Member of the Board of Revenue, the functions assigned 
to a Settlement Commissioner by or under the said Act, 
in regard to the management, custody and disposal of the 
acquired evacuee properties, agricultural lands, shops and 
vacant sites forming part of the Compensation Pool in the 
State of Andhra Pradesh. 

[No. 25/2/73-SS IV] 
D. N. ASIZA, Under Secy.
